When my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I do get a bit nervous… like I might start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know that may sound silly, doctor, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself actively avoiding eating certain foods or reducing your meals because you're afraid of gaining weight, or has your lack of appetite been more about not really feeling interested in food?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s a bit of both, but… if I’m honest, it isn’t just not being interested in food. I do get uneasy with certain things. Like when my wife puts butter on something, or gives me what feels like a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on. I know that probably sounds a bit particular, but I just feel better if I keep things under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or exercised excessively to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I don’t… I don’t do things like that. I’ve never made myself sick or anything. I suppose I just try to keep my portions sensible. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are overweight or do you have a strong fear of gaining weight, even if others might tell you that you’re not?\n\nPatient: I don’t exactly think of myself as overweight, no… not really. But I do get frightened of putting weight back on, I suppose. Like if my wife puts butter on something, or serves me what looks like a full plate, I feel nervous that I’ll lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may not sound very reasonable, doctor, but that’s how it comes over me.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or gaining weight caused you any significant distress or affected your daily life, like making it hard to eat with others or causing arguments with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say it’s stopped me living my life, doctor. I still do most of the chores around the house and try not to make a fuss. But my wife has started getting worried when we go out. Sometimes she’ll cut an outing short, or say I should sit out one of the community tours if she thinks I haven’t eaten enough beforehand. I suppose that has been a bit upsetting for both of us… but I don’t mean to cause trouble.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t always see it that way myself, if I’m honest. Being lighter feels like it helps me get around the garden more easily, and keep up with my walks. But yes, I suppose they do think it’s lower than it ought to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are currently underweight, or do you still see yourself as having weight to lose or needing to stay very careful with your eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel underweight, if I’m being honest… I mean, I know people keep saying it, and I don’t want to be difficult. I do think I need to stay careful with eating, though… just not overdo things.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or weight caused you to avoid social situations, such as meals with friends or family gatherings, even if you wanted to attend?\n\nPatient: Not really avoid them on purpose, no… I do still like going along when we can. I try to keep up with the chores and our usual things.\n\nIt’s more that my wife gets worried. It doesn’t seem like such a big thing to me, but she thought it was worth asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight or feel like you need to control your weight in any specific way?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I worry all the time, exactly. I just like to be careful, if that makes sense. When my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I do get a bit nervous… like I might start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know that may sound silly, doctor, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself actively avoiding eating certain foods or reducing your meals because you're afraid of gaining weight, or has your lack of appetite been more about not really feeling interested in food?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s a bit of both, but… if I’m honest, it isn’t just not being interested in food. I do get uneasy with certain things. Like when my wife puts butter on something, or gives me what feels like a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on. I know that probably sounds a bit particular, but I just feel better if I keep things under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or exercised excessively to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I don’t… I don’t do things like that. I’ve never made myself sick or anything. I suppose I just try to keep my portions sensible. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are overweight or do you have a strong fear of gaining weight, even if others might tell you that you’re not?\n\nPatient: I don’t exactly think of myself as overweight, no… not really. But I do get frightened of putting weight back on, I suppose. Like if my wife puts butter on something, or serves me what looks like a full plate, I feel nervous that I’ll lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may not sound very reasonable, doctor, but that’s how it comes over me.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or gaining weight caused you any significant distress or affected your daily life, like making it hard to eat with others or causing arguments with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say it’s stopped me living my life, doctor. I still do most of the chores around the house and try not to make a fuss. But my wife has started getting worried when we go out. Sometimes she’ll cut an outing short, or say I should sit out one of the community tours if she thinks I haven’t eaten enough beforehand. I suppose that has been a bit upsetting for both of us… but I don’t mean to cause trouble.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you noticed yourself losing a significant amount of weight recently, or do you think your current weight is significantly lower than what would be expected for someone your age and height?\n\nPatient: Well… my doctor has mentioned that the number on the scale has got a bit too low. I don’t always see it that way myself, if I’m honest. Being lighter feels like it helps me get around the garden more easily, and keep up with my walks. But yes, I suppose they do think it’s lower than it ought to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are currently underweight, or do you still see yourself as having weight to lose or needing to stay very careful with your eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel underweight, if I’m being honest… I mean, I know people keep saying it, and I don’t want to be difficult. But at my age, surely it’s normal not to eat quite as much as I used to, isn’t it?\n\nI’m still doing my bits around the house, and I can manage the tours and my usual chores, so I suppose I don’t quite see why everyone is making such a fuss. I do think I need to stay careful with eating, though… just not overdo things.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or weight caused you to avoid social situations, such as meals with friends or family gatherings, even if you wanted to attend?\n\nPatient: Not really avoid them on purpose, no… I do still like going along when we can. I try to keep up with the chores and our usual things.\n\nIt’s more that my wife gets worried. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut an outing short, or tell me maybe I should sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that does mean we miss things now and then… but I don’t want it to sound like I’m trying to be difficult.\n\nDoctor: Has your worry about eating or your need to control your food intake ever made it hard for you to function normally, like causing problems with your relationship, your mood, or making it hard to take part in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it stops me functioning normally, exactly… I do still keep up with most of the chores and the things I’m meant to do.\n\nBut, well, my wife does get concerned. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut our outings short, or she’ll say I ought to sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that has made things a bit awkward now and then. I don’t mean to cause any trouble with it, but yes… it has affected what we do a little.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Outside of what your wife does or says, do you ever feel distressed or upset about your eating habits or the way you feel around food?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m upset all the time, doctor. It’s not like I’m trying to make a drama of it.\n\nBut I suppose I do get a bit anxious when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate. I start worrying that if I eat like that, I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may sound silly… but yes, that does make me nervous.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

I do think I need to stay careful with eating, though… just not overdo things.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or weight caused you to avoid social situations, such as meals with friends or family gatherings, even if you wanted to attend?\n\nPatient: Not really avoid them on purpose, no… I do still like going along when we can. I try to keep up with the chores and our usual things.\n\nIt’s more that my wife gets worried. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut an outing short, or tell me maybe I should sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that does mean we miss things now and then… but I don’t want it to sound like I’m trying to be difficult.\n\nDoctor: Has your worry about eating or your need to control your food intake ever made it hard for you to function normally, like causing problems with your relationship, your mood, or making it hard to take part in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it stops me functioning normally, exactly… I do still keep up with most of the chores and the things I’m meant to do.\n\nBut, well, my wife does get concerned. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut our outings short, or she’ll say I ought to sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that has made things a bit awkward now and then. I don’t mean to cause any trouble with it, but yes… it has affected what we do a little.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Outside of what your wife does or says, do you ever feel distressed or upset about your eating habits or the way you feel around food?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m upset all the time, doctor. It’s not like I’m trying to make a drama of it.\n\nBut I suppose I do get a bit anxious when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate. I start worrying that if I eat like that, I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may sound silly… but yes, that does make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Even though others may tell you that you're underweight, do you still feel a strong need to restrict your food intake because of a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I don’t like to make it sound too serious, but when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous.\n\nIt’s that feeling that if I eat too much, I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know people say I’m too thin, but that worry is still there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not like I’m trying to make a drama of it.\n\nBut I suppose I do get a bit anxious when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate. I start worrying that if I eat like that, I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may sound silly… but yes, that does make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Even though others may tell you that you're underweight, do you still feel a strong need to restrict your food intake because of a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I don’t like to make it sound too serious, but when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous.\n\nIt’s that feeling that if I eat too much, I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know people say I’m too thin, but that worry is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about gaining weight or feel the need to control your eating, is it mostly because you feel like you need to look a certain way, or is it more about how you feel when you eat certain foods or feel full?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s… a bit of both, if that makes sense. I don’t think I’m trying to look like anything special, not at my age. But I do notice things.\n\nIf my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, I suppose. Disappointed in myself. I know that may not be the best way to think about it, doctor, but that’s how it feels.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t want to overstate it, doctor… but yes, I suppose it does affect how I see myself a bit.

When my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done something right, if that makes sense. Like I’ve kept things in order. But if I catch myself in the mirror and my stomach looks even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, really. Disappointed in myself.

I know that probably isn’t the most sensible way to judge things, but that’s honestly how it feels.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, thank you for coming in. What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose I’m here mostly because my wife thought I should come. She worries I’m not eating enough, but I don’t know… I feel like at my age big meals aren’t really necessary. Most days I’ll have coffee and some toast in the morning, and then maybe soup or vegetables later on. It doesn’t seem like such a big thing to me, but she thought it was worth asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight or feel like you need to control your weight in any specific way?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I worry all the time, exactly. I just like to be careful, if that makes sense. When my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I do get a bit nervous… like I might start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know that may sound silly, doctor, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself actively avoiding eating certain foods or reducing your meals because you're afraid of gaining weight, or has your lack of appetite been more about not really feeling interested in food?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s a bit of both, but… if I’m honest, it isn’t just not being interested in food. I do get uneasy with certain things. Like when my wife puts butter on something, or gives me what feels like a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on. I know that probably sounds a bit particular, but I just feel better if I keep things under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or exercised excessively to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I don’t… I don’t do things like that. I’ve never made myself sick or anything. I suppose I just try to keep my portions sensible. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are overweight or do you have a strong fear of gaining weight, even if others might tell you that you’re not?\n\nPatient: I don’t exactly think of myself as overweight, no… not really. But I do get frightened of putting weight back on, I suppose. Like if my wife puts butter on something, or serves me what looks like a full plate, I feel nervous that I’ll lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may not sound very reasonable, doctor, but that’s how it comes over me.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or gaining weight caused you any significant distress or affected your daily life, like making it hard to eat with others or causing arguments with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say it’s stopped me living my life, doctor. I still do most of the chores around the house and try not to make a fuss. But my wife has started getting worried when we go out. Sometimes she’ll cut an outing short, or say I should sit out one of the community tours if she thinks I haven’t eaten enough beforehand. I suppose that has been a bit upsetting for both of us… but I don’t mean to cause trouble.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you noticed yourself losing a significant amount of weight recently, or do you think your current weight is significantly lower than what would be expected for someone your age and height?\n\nPatient: Well… my doctor has mentioned that the number on the scale has got a bit too low. I don’t always see it that way myself, if I’m honest. Being lighter feels like it helps me get around the garden more easily, and keep up with my walks. But yes, I suppose they do think it’s lower than it ought to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are currently underweight, or do you still see yourself as having weight to lose or needing to stay very careful with your eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel underweight, if I’m being honest… I mean, I know people keep saying it, and I don’t want to be difficult. But at my age, surely it’s normal not to eat quite as much as I used to, isn’t it?\n\nI’m still doing my bits around the house, and I can manage the tours and my usual chores, so I suppose I don’t quite see why everyone is making such a fuss. I do think I need to stay careful with eating, though… just not overdo things.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or weight caused you to avoid social situations, such as meals with friends or family gatherings, even if you wanted to attend?\n\nPatient: Not really avoid them on purpose, no… I do still like going along when we can. I try to keep up with the chores and our usual things.\n\nIt’s more that my wife gets worried. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut an outing short, or tell me maybe I should sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that does mean we miss things now and then… but I don’t want it to sound like I’m trying to be difficult.\n\nDoctor: Has your worry about eating or your need to control your food intake ever made it hard for you to function normally, like causing problems with your relationship, your mood, or making it hard to take part in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it stops me functioning normally, exactly… I do still keep up with most of the chores and the things I’m meant to do.\n\nBut, well, my wife does get concerned. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut our outings short, or she’ll say I ought to sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that has made things a bit awkward now and then. I don’t mean to cause any trouble with it, but yes… it has affected what we do a little.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Outside of what your wife does or says, do you ever feel distressed or upset about your eating habits or the way you feel around food?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m upset all the time, doctor. It’s not like I’m trying to make a drama of it.\n\nBut I suppose I do get a bit anxious when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate. I start worrying that if I eat like that, I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may sound silly… but yes, that does make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Even though others may tell you that you're underweight, do you still feel a strong need to restrict your food intake because of a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I don’t like to make it sound too serious, but when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous.\n\nIt’s that feeling that if I eat too much, I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know people say I’m too thin, but that worry is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about gaining weight or feel the need to control your eating, is it mostly because you feel like you need to look a certain way, or is it more about how you feel when you eat certain foods or feel full?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s… a bit of both, if that makes sense. I don’t like to make it sound too serious, but when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous.\n\nIt’s that feeling that if I eat too much, I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know people say I’m too thin, but that worry is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about gaining weight or feel the need to control your eating, is it mostly because you feel like you need to look a certain way, or is it more about how you feel when you eat certain foods or feel full?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s… a bit of both, if that makes sense. I don’t think I’m trying to look like anything special, not at my age. But I do notice things.\n\nIf my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, I suppose. Disappointed in myself. I know that may not be the best way to think about it, doctor, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body shape or size has been affecting the way you see yourself, or do you feel like certain parts of your body are still too big even though others might say you're too thin?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t want to overstate it, doctor… but yes, I suppose it does affect how I see myself a bit.\n\nWhen my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done something right, if that makes sense. Like I’ve kept things in order. But if I catch myself in the mirror and my stomach looks even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, really. What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose I’m here mostly because my wife thought I should come. She worries I’m not eating enough, but I don’t know… I feel like at my age big meals aren’t really necessary. Most days I’ll have coffee and some toast in the morning, and then maybe soup or vegetables later on. It doesn’t seem like such a big thing to me, but she thought it was worth asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight or feel like you need to control your weight in any specific way?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I worry all the time, exactly. I just like to be careful, if that makes sense. When my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I do get a bit nervous… like I might start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know that may sound silly, doctor, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself actively avoiding eating certain foods or reducing your meals because you're afraid of gaining weight, or has your lack of appetite been more about not really feeling interested in food?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s a bit of both, but… if I’m honest, it isn’t just not being interested in food. I do get uneasy with certain things. Like when my wife puts butter on something, or gives me what feels like a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on. I know that probably sounds a bit particular, but I just feel better if I keep things under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or exercised excessively to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I don’t… I don’t do things like that. I’ve never made myself sick or anything. I suppose I just try to keep my portions sensible. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are overweight or do you have a strong fear of gaining weight, even if others might tell you that you’re not?\n\nPatient: I don’t exactly think of myself as overweight, no… not really. But I do get frightened of putting weight back on, I suppose. Like if my wife puts butter on something, or serves me what looks like a full plate, I feel nervous that I’ll lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may not sound very reasonable, doctor, but that’s how it comes over me.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or gaining weight caused you any significant distress or affected your daily life, like making it hard to eat with others or causing arguments with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say it’s stopped me living my life, doctor. I still do most of the chores around the house and try not to make a fuss. But my wife has started getting worried when we go out. Sometimes she’ll cut an outing short, or say I should sit out one of the community tours if she thinks I haven’t eaten enough beforehand. I suppose that has been a bit upsetting for both of us… but I don’t mean to cause trouble.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you noticed yourself losing a significant amount of weight recently, or do you think your current weight is significantly lower than what would be expected for someone your age and height?\n\nPatient: Well… my doctor has mentioned that the number on the scale has got a bit too low. I don’t always see it that way myself, if I’m honest. Being lighter feels like it helps me get around the garden more easily, and keep up with my walks. But yes, I suppose they do think it’s lower than it ought to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are currently underweight, or do you still see yourself as having weight to lose or needing to stay very careful with your eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel underweight, if I’m being honest… I mean, I know people keep saying it, and I don’t want to be difficult. But at my age, surely it’s normal not to eat quite as much as I used to, isn’t it?\n\nI’m still doing my bits around the house, and I can manage the tours and my usual chores, so I suppose I don’t quite see why everyone is making such a fuss. I do think I need to stay careful with eating, though… just not overdo things.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or weight caused you to avoid social situations, such as meals with friends or family gatherings, even if you wanted to attend?\n\nPatient: Not really avoid them on purpose, no… I do still like going along when we can. I don’t mean to cause any trouble with it, but yes… it has affected what we do a little.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Outside of what your wife does or says, do you ever feel distressed or upset about your eating habits or the way you feel around food?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m upset all the time, doctor. It’s not like I’m trying to make a drama of it.\n\nBut I suppose I do get a bit anxious when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate. I start worrying that if I eat like that, I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may sound silly… but yes, that does make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Even though others may tell you that you're underweight, do you still feel a strong need to restrict your food intake because of a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I don’t like to make it sound too serious, but when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous.\n\nIt’s that feeling that if I eat too much, I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know people say I’m too thin, but that worry is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about gaining weight or feel the need to control your eating, is it mostly because you feel like you need to look a certain way, or is it more about how you feel when you eat certain foods or feel full?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s… a bit of both, if that makes sense. I don’t think I’m trying to look like anything special, not at my age. But I do notice things.\n\nIf my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, I suppose. Disappointed in myself. I know that may not be the best way to think about it, doctor, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body shape or size has been affecting the way you see yourself, or do you feel like certain parts of your body are still too big even though others might say you're too thin?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t want to overstate it, doctor… but yes, I suppose it does affect how I see myself a bit.\n\nWhen my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done something right, if that makes sense. Like I’ve kept things in order. But if I catch myself in the mirror and my stomach looks even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, really. Disappointed in myself.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the most sensible way to judge things, but that’s honestly how it feels.\n\nDoctor: When you restrict your eating or feel nervous about eating certain foods, is it mostly because of a fear of gaining weight, or is it more about how the food makes you feel physically or other reasons?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the weight, if I’m being honest… though I don’t like to make too much of it.\n\nWhen my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a plate that looks very full, I get anxious. I start thinking, well, if I eat all that, I’ll put weight back on, and then I’ve lost the control I’ve tried to keep. Does that make sense, doctor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I know that may not sound very reasonable, doctor, but that’s how it comes over me.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or gaining weight caused you any significant distress or affected your daily life, like making it hard to eat with others or causing arguments with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say it’s stopped me living my life, doctor. I still do most of the chores around the house and try not to make a fuss. But my wife has started getting worried when we go out. Sometimes she’ll cut an outing short, or say I should sit out one of the community tours if she thinks I haven’t eaten enough beforehand. I suppose that has been a bit upsetting for both of us… but I don’t mean to cause trouble.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you noticed yourself losing a significant amount of weight recently, or do you think your current weight is significantly lower than what would be expected for someone your age and height?\n\nPatient: Well… my doctor has mentioned that the number on the scale has got a bit too low. I don’t always see it that way myself, if I’m honest. Being lighter feels like it helps me get around the garden more easily, and keep up with my walks. But yes, I suppose they do think it’s lower than it ought to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are currently underweight, or do you still see yourself as having weight to lose or needing to stay very careful with your eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel underweight, if I’m being honest… I mean, I know people keep saying it, and I don’t want to be difficult. But at my age, surely it’s normal not to eat quite as much as I used to, isn’t it?\n\nI’m still doing my bits around the house, and I can manage the tours and my usual chores, so I suppose I don’t quite see why everyone is making such a fuss. I do think I need to stay careful with eating, though… just not overdo things.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about eating or weight caused you to avoid social situations, such as meals with friends or family gatherings, even if you wanted to attend?\n\nPatient: Not really avoid them on purpose, no… I do still like going along when we can. I try to keep up with the chores and our usual things.\n\nIt’s more that my wife gets worried. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut an outing short, or tell me maybe I should sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that does mean we miss things now and then… but I don’t want it to sound like I’m trying to be difficult.\n\nDoctor: Has your worry about eating or your need to control your food intake ever made it hard for you to function normally, like causing problems with your relationship, your mood, or making it hard to take part in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it stops me functioning normally, exactly… I do still keep up with most of the chores and the things I’m meant to do.\n\nBut, well, my wife does get concerned. If she thinks I haven’t eaten enough, she’ll sometimes cut our outings short, or she’ll say I ought to sit out one of the community tours. I suppose that has made things a bit awkward now and then. I don’t mean to cause any trouble with it, but yes… it has affected what we do a little.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Outside of what your wife does or says, do you ever feel distressed or upset about your eating habits or the way you feel around food?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m upset all the time, doctor. It’s not like I’m trying to make a drama of it.\n\nBut I suppose I do get a bit anxious when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate. I start worrying that if I eat like that, I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. I know that may sound silly… but yes, that does make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Even though others may tell you that you're underweight, do you still feel a strong need to restrict your food intake because of a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I don’t like to make it sound too serious, but when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous.\n\nIt’s that feeling that if I eat too much, I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. I know people say I’m too thin, but that worry is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about gaining weight or feel the need to control your eating, is it mostly because you feel like you need to look a certain way, or is it more about how you feel when you eat certain foods or feel full?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s… a bit of both, if that makes sense. I don’t think I’m trying to look like anything special, not at my age. But I do notice things.\n\nIf my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, I suppose. Disappointed in myself. I know that may not be the best way to think about it, doctor, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body shape or size has been affecting the way you see yourself, or do you feel like certain parts of your body are still too big even though others might say you're too thin?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t want to overstate it, doctor… but yes, I suppose it does affect how I see myself a bit.\n\nWhen my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done something right, if that makes sense. Like I’ve kept things in order. But if I catch myself in the mirror and my stomach looks even a little rounded, I feel… sloppy, really. Disappointed in myself.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the most sensible way to judge things, but that’s honestly how it feels.\n\nDoctor: When you restrict your eating or feel nervous about eating certain foods, is it mostly because of a fear of gaining weight, or is it more about how the food makes you feel physically or other reasons?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the weight, if I’m being honest… though I don’t like to make too much of it.\n\nWhen my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a plate that looks very full, I get anxious. I start thinking, well, if I eat all that, I’ll put weight back on, and then I’ve lost the control I’ve tried to keep. Does that make sense, doctor?\n\nDoctor: When you worry about gaining weight or feel the need to control your eating, is it mostly because you see yourself as larger than you are, or is it more about not wanting to feel physically uncomfortable from eating certain foods or being full?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s more about how I see myself, yes… though I don’t want to sound vain or foolish.\n\nIt’s not so much one particular food making me feel ill or anything. It’s that if my belt feels a little looser, I feel as though I’ve done well, like I’ve kept myself in check. But if I see my stomach looking even the least bit rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, and disappointed in myself.\n\nI know that may not be the right way to look at it, doctor, but that’s how it tends to feel.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
